import SystemTableCloud from '@site/docs/_snippets/_system_table_cloud.md';

<SystemTableCloud/>

## Description {#description}

Contains history of metrics values from tables `system.metrics` and `system.events`, periodically flushed to disk.

Profile events and current metrics are stored in 128 bucket columns `metrics_0` ... `metrics_127` of type [Map](/sql-reference/data-types/map)([Enum16](/sql-reference/data-types/enum), [Int64](/sql-reference/data-types/int-uint)). Each metric belongs to exactly one bucket, and the key of the map is the name of the metric. Zero values are not stored: reading a missing key returns `0`.

In addition, every metric is exposed through an `ALIAS` column named as the metric itself (for example, `ProfileEvent_Query`, `CurrentMetric_TCPConnection`), so queries do not need to know the bucket layout. Reading such a column reads only the single bucket that contains the metric. Profile events are stored as increments during the collection interval, while current metrics are stored as values at the moment of collection.

## Columns {#columns}

- `hostname` ([LowCardinality(String)](/sql-reference/data-types/lowcardinality)) — Hostname of the server executing the query.
- `event_date` ([Date](/sql-reference/data-types/date)) — Event date.
- `event_time` ([DateTime](/sql-reference/data-types/datetime)) — Event time.
- `event_time_microseconds` ([DateTime64(6)](/sql-reference/data-types/datetime64)) — Event time with microseconds resolution.

- `histograms.metric` ([Array(LowCardinality(String))](/sql-reference/data-types/array)) — Names of histogram families snapshotted in this row.
- `histograms.labels` ([Array(Map(LowCardinality(String), LowCardinality(String)))](/sql-reference/data-types/array)) — Per-entry label maps.
- `histograms.histogram` ([Array(Map(Float64, UInt64))](/sql-reference/data-types/array)) — Per-entry cumulative bucket counts keyed by upper bound; +Inf is the final entry and equals count.
- `histograms.count` ([Array(UInt64)](/sql-reference/data-types/array)) — Per-entry total observation counts.
- `histograms.sum` ([Array(Float64)](/sql-reference/data-types/array)) — Per-entry sums of observed values.

## Example {#example}

Metrics are usually queried through their `ALIAS` columns:

```sql
SELECT event_time, ProfileEvent_Query, CurrentMetric_TCPConnection
FROM system.metric_log
ORDER BY event_time DESC
LIMIT 3;
```

```text
┌──────────event_time─┬─ProfileEvent_Query─┬─CurrentMetric_TCPConnection─┐
│ 2026-08-19 00:28:44 │                  2 │                           0 │
│ 2026-08-19 00:28:43 │                  3 │                           1 │
│ 2026-08-19 00:28:32 │                  3 │                           0 │
└─────────────────────┴────────────────────┴─────────────────────────────┘
```

The underlying bucket columns can be read directly (zero values are not stored):

```sql
SELECT event_time, metrics_0 FROM system.metric_log ORDER BY event_time DESC LIMIT 1 FORMAT Vertical;
```

```text
Row 1:
──────
event_time: 2026-08-19 00:28:44
metrics_0:  {'ProfileEvent_Query':2,'ProfileEvent_SelectQuery':2,'ProfileEvent_InitialQuery':2,'ProfileEvent_InitialSelectQuery':2,'ProfileEvent_QueriesWithSubqueries':2,'ProfileEvent_SelectQueriesWithSubqueries':2}
```

**Schema**
This table can be configured with different schema types using the XML tag `<schema_type>`. The default schema type is `wide`, which stores all metrics of a point in time as a single row, bucketed into 128 `Map(Enum16(...), Int64)` columns with a per-metric `ALIAS` column for each metric. This schema is the most performant and efficient for reads of a single metric over a time range.

The `transposed` schema stores data in a format similar to `system.asynchronous_metric_log`, where metrics and events are stored as rows. This schema is useful for low-resource setups because it reduces resource consumption during merges.

**Histograms**

Each row also carries a snapshot of every registered histogram metric in a `histograms` Nested column with fields `metric`, `labels`, `histogram`, `count`, and `sum`. Bucket counts are cumulative since server startup. By default, histograms whose total `count` is zero are not emitted, and zero-counter buckets within an emitted histogram are omitted from the `histogram` map; set `system_metric_log_show_zero_values_in_histograms = 1` (in the default user profile) to keep all histograms and all buckets.

Example query:

```sql
SELECT h.metric, h.labels, h.histogram, h.count, h.sum
FROM system.metric_log
ARRAY JOIN histograms AS h
WHERE h.metric = 'keeper_response_time_ms' AND h.labels['operation_type'] = 'readonly'
ORDER BY event_time DESC
LIMIT 1;
```
